US DATA: Ethanol production hits 10-month high following plant turnarounds
Output rises for fifth straight week to 1.143 mil b/d
Total stocks dip to 24.434 mil barrels
Blending inputs remain near multimonth lows
US ethanol production rose to a 10-month high in the week to Oct. 30, while total stocks ticked lower, according to Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4.
Ethanol production rose 25,000 barrels/day to 1.143 million b/d, reaching its highest level since Jan. 9 when output hit an all-time high of 1.196 million b/d. Compared to the year-ago week, production was 20,000 b/d, or 1.78%, higher. Production rose for the fifth consecutive week as output gradually ramped up following peak plant turnarounds in late September.
Total stocks fell 89,000 barrels, or 0.36%, to 24.434 million barrels. Compared to the year-ago week, total stocks were 1.779

million barrels, or 7.85%, higher. Stock draws were reported across the reporting regions, excluding a 2.41% build on the East Coast.
Midwest stocks fell 100,000 barrels to 9.697 million barrels, coming off a six-week high reached the previous week. Compared to the year-ago week, regional stocks were 546,000 barrels, or 5.97%, higher. The Midwest hosts the most liquid trading hub in the US at the Kinder Morgan terminal near Chicago as well as most of the ethanol production plants in the US.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Chicago Terminal ethanol benchmark at $1.8820/gal on Nov. 3. In October, the benchmark tumbled 11.50% as pressure was drawn from mounting supplies and reduced driving demand, along with a sharp fall in corn prices as the harvest nears completion.
East Coast stocks rose 164,000 barrels to 6.981 million barrels, up from a three-month low hit the previous week. Compared to the year-ago week, regional stocks were 485,000 barrels, or 7.47%, higher. The East Coast hosts large consumption hubs and the New York Harbor market, a key hub for ethanol trading.
Platts assessed New York Harbor ITT Sewaren at $2.0675/ gal, while front-month November FOB barges were assessed at $2.05/gal on Nov. 3. Tradable levels for spot New York Harbor have remained elevated relative to Chicago, as a bid for this week ITT Sewaren/this week Chicago Terminal was last heard at plus 16 cents.
Gulf Coast stocks fell 134,000 barrels to a six-month low of 4.864 million barrels. Compared to the year-ago week, regional stocks were 698,000 barrels, or 16.75%, higher. The region is the most common origin for exports, but also hosts large consumption hubs.
According to the EIA, weekly ethanol exports rose 31,000 b/d to 147,000 b/d. Year to date, exports are averaging 145,000 b/d. In 2025, the US set an annual record for ethanol exports, averaging 131,750 b/d per week.
West Coast stocks fell 19,000 barrels to 2.553 million barrels. Compared to the year-ago week, regional stocks were 71,000 barrels, or 2.86%, higher.
The four-week rolling average of the refiner and blender net ethanol input held unchanged at a six-month low of 899,000 b/d. Meanwhile, the weekly average fell 8,000 b/d to 899,000 b/d. Compared to the year-ago week, the weekly average was 5,000 b/d, or 0.55%, lower.
The four-week rolling average of gasoline demand, represented by product supplied, rose 10,000 b/d to 8.639 million b/d, rebounding from an eight-month low hit the previous week. Meanwhile, the weekly average rose 450,000 b/d to 8.808 million b/d, also rebounding from an eight-month low. Compared to the year-ago week, the weekly average was 66,000 b/d, or 0.74%, lower.
The four-week rolling average of the ethanol blending rate, calculated by dividing the refiner and blender ethanol input by gasoline demand, fell from 10.42% to 10.41%.
US ethanol days of supply -- calculated by dividing inventories by the refiner and blender net ethanol input - rose from 27.04 days to 27.18 days.
-- Melvin Lee

Platts European Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
Gasoline prices drop $32.25/mt amid weak crude
Buying interest fades as year's end approaches
Oxygenate blending economics improve
European gasoline markets tumbled on Nov. 4, with volatile flat prices selling off alongside weaker crude, while buying interest remained subdued across European markets.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed FOB ARA Eurobob gasoline barges down $32.25/metric ton at $918/mt, while the equivalent cash differential strengthened $1.25/mt to $14.75/mt.
E5 Eurobob oxy gasoline premiums strengthened relative to E10 non-oxy gasoline amid improved economics for oxygenate blending, despite weakness in outright gasoline prices.
The relative strength in E5 coincided with a sharp decline in the cost of MTBE against finished gasoline, improving the economics of blending the oxygenate into the gasoline pool. Platts assessed the FOB ARA MTBE premium over the M1 E5 swap at $502.50/mt, down $44/mt day over day.
ETBE also became cheaper relative to MTBE, with the

physical FOB ARA ETBE/MTBE spread falling $10/mt to a $199.75/ mt premium. The spread has narrowed considerably from a $269.75/mt premium a week earlier, adding to improved relative economics for oxygenate blending.
The improved blending economics came against a backdrop of limited overall buying interest, with one market source describing little interest following a recent market "flush out" and participants largely "waiting for the year-end." The source also pointed to geopolitical uncertainty as complicating the near-term outlook.
Platts assessed the front-month FOB ARA Eurobob barge swap at $903.25/mt, down $34/mt, with the equivalent crack down 2 cents/barrel at $12.35/b.
The November/December spread rose $1.50/mt to $17.50/mt, while December/January spread rose 50 cents/mt to $7.25/mt.
The November Mediterranean/North gasoline differential, the spread between the November FOB Mediterranean gasoline swap and the equivalent FOB ARA Eurobob barge swap, was unchanged at a $30/mt premium, while the December differential rose $2.50/mt to a $28/mt premium.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Friedrich Gutsche

ICE front-month Singapore jet fuel open interest rises 29.95% MOM in Oct
Open interest for Singapore regrade up 12.97% MOM
Front-month crack spread open interest grows 7.6%
Total open interest for all front-month November Singapore jet fuel contracts -- including jet/kerosene and jet kerosene/gasoil 10 parts per million sulfur -- on the Intercontinental Exchange rose 29.95% month over month to 24.413 million barrels in October, the latest ICE data showed Nov. 3.
The open interest for front-month November Singapore jet fuel/kerosene contracts rose 56.10% month over month to 11.603 million barrels, reaching the highest since August, when open interest stood at 13.892 million barrels, the data showed.
Tracking the rise, the open interest for the front-month November Singapore regrade rose 12.97% month over month to 12.470 million barrels in October, rebounding from a four-month low in September, the data showed.
The open interest for the front-month November Singapore jet fuel/kerosene against the front-month Dubai swap crack also rose 7.59% month over month to 340,000 barrels in October.
The rise in open interest during the month coincided with heavy jet fuel/kerosene supply in Asia, which has weighed on market sentiment.
Regional balances will likely remain long following China's release of its fourth batch of export quotas this year, as market participants expect the move to prompt the country's refiners to raise runs and boost shipments.

Looking downstream, global air passenger demand -- measured in revenue passenger kilometers -- fell 0.5% year over year in September, according to the latest data from the International Air Transport Association, driven by weak traffic in the Middle East as conflict within the region persists, while elevated jet fuel prices continue to curtail travel demand.

Switzerland allows for second time release of jet fuel stocks
Up to 50,000 cubic meters draws allowed until Nov. 29 Refinery restrictions and rail issues cause strain Rhine water levels disrupt oil products transport

Switzerland has authorized for a second time the release of jet fuel stocks due to temporary shortages, the Swiss Federal Office for National Economic Supply, or FONES, said Nov. 3.
Previously, Switzerland allowed the release of jet fuel stocks between Oct. 15 and Nov. 1 to maintain supplies at Zurich Airport.
In its latest decision, the country allows up to 50,000 cubic meters to be drawn until Nov. 29. Including the already authorized draws of up to 30,000 cubic meters, the total volume represents 17.4% of the obligatory jet fuel stocks, FONES said.
The release has been attributed to restrictions in refinery operations in southern France and in Germany, further compounded by rail freight disruptions.
Market sources have reported weather-related disruptions to refinery operations in southern France over the recent weeks.
"Despite low demand in November 2026, the airports of Geneva and Zurich are therefore relying on withdrawals from mandatory reserves," FONES said.

FONES also reiterated that Switzerland's supply of oil products had been negatively impacted for months due to lower Rhine water levels, which had put additional strain on the use of alternative transportation.
Currently, Switzerland also allows the use of diesel stocks until Nov. 22.
-- Elza Turner, Nikos Batagiannis, Jan Kedzior
Guangzhou project approved to produce 1 million mt/y of SAF feedstock oil
Pyrolysis converts biomass into SAF feedstock oil
Construction scheduled for 2027-31 in two phases
A 3.68 billion yuan biomass project in Guangzhou's Nansha district in southern China has received provincial filing approval to produce up to 1 million metric tons/year of sustainable aviation fuel feedstock oil, according to a Guangdong government investment platform.
The Zhongguang Zhitou Nansha Headquarters Base project was approved on Nov. 2 by the administrative approval bureau of the Guangzhou Nansha Economic and Technological Development Zone, following a filing submitted on Oct. 30, according to the Guangdong Provincial Investment Project Online Approval and Supervision Platform on Nov. 3.
The project will use high-temperature pyrolysis and cracking to convert biomass into pyrolysis oil for SAF production, along with biomass carbon black and wood vinegar as co-products, according to the filing.
The 1 million mt/y figure refers to SAF feedstock oil rather than finished jet fuel. The pyrolysis oil would need further upgrading at a refinery, including hydroprocessing, before it could be turned into aviation fuel. The filing did not specify where the upgrading would take place, the expected SAF yield from the feedstock oil or the type of biomass to be used.
Two-phase build-out The project plans to install 10 standardized biomass feedstock
oil production lines in the first phase and 40 in the second, bringing the total to 50 lines.
After phase one, output is expected to reach 200,000 mt/y of SAF feedstock oil, 143,000 mt/y of biomass carbon black and 85,800 mt/y of wood vinegar. After phase two, capacity would increase to 1 million mt/y, 715,000 mt/y and 429,000 mt/y, respectively, for a total of 2.144 million mt/y.
Construction is scheduled to begin in January 2027 and be completed in December 2031. The filing did not provide separate completion dates for the two phases.
The project will be built in leased factory buildings at the Dagang Advanced Manufacturing Base in Nansha, covering 65,900 square meters of land with 130,000 square meter of floor space.
The developer, Zhongguang Zhitou Waste Resource Comprehensive Utilization (Guangzhou) Co., was registered on Sept. 7, with registered capital of 10 million yuan, according to business

Pyrolysis-based routes to aviation fuel remain far less commercially established than the hydroprocessed esters and fatty acids pathway, which dominates current Chinese SAF production and uses waste oils such as used cooking oil. Approval of the filing allows the project to proceed with preparatory work but does not indicate that financing or a final investment decision is in place.

The SAF FOB China premium was at $723.25/mt over the Platts jet kerosene FOB Singapore forward curve (MOPS) on Nov. 3, up $2.75/mt from Nov. 2.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Jet Daily Market Analysis
Chinese refineries likely to increase Nov clean oil product exports
Regrade softens amid long supply
The Asian jet fuel/kerosene market structure remained rangebound Nov. 4 amid mixed geopolitical headlines.
Platts assessed the balance November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at minus 39 cents/b at the Asian close Nov. 3, down 21 cents/b day over day.
In midmorning Asian trading Nov. 4, the spread rebounded to plus 13 cents/b, according to broker pegs.
US and Iran are reviewing fresh proposals from mediators to bridge their differences and end the war, according to media reports. US President Donald Trump told the Financial Times Nov. 3 that there are no immediate plans for US strikes on Iran.
Meanwhile, Iran says a "maximum deterrence" plan is ready against any new attack by the US, according to media reports.
The dual messaging has left market participants uncertain about the durability of any de-escalation. Just hours before the midterm elections, the US renewed military action targeted at Iran, launching strikes at Qeshm Island, with several explosions reported. According to the US, the strikes were in retaliation for continued Iranian attacks.
Nonetheless, weak fundamentals amid an overhang of cargoes within Asia will likely keep a lid on prices.
Chinese refineries are likely to increase their total clean oil product exports to about 4 million metric tons in November after being awarded new quotas, three Beijing-based sources close to the matter said Nov. 3.
The sources said jet fuel volumes would increase to about 2.4 million mt, from estimations of about 2 million mt before the allocation of new quotas at the end of October.

Reflecting the weakness, brokers pegged the balance November swaps regrade -- the value jet fuel commands over 10 ppm sulfur gasoil -- at minus $1.02/b midmorning Nov. 4, down from the Platts assessment of minus 98 cents/b at the market close Nov. 3.
Over the same period, the front-month December swaps regrade fell from plus 83 cents/b to plus 55 cents/b.
In the physical segment, the Platts-assessed FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene cargo cash differential widened 11 cents/b day over day to minus $1.21/b against the Mean of Platts Singapore jet fuel/kerosene assessments Nov. 3.
Separately, global product tanker rates are surging as geopolitical disruptions, strong refining margins and depleted fuel inventories drive demand for shipping capacity, Ardmore said Nov. 3, after reporting recent bookings at more than twice the levels achieved the prior quarter.
The strength comes before the traditional winter market fully develops, with CEO Gernot Ruppelt pointing to tight Atlantic product markets, elevated refining margins and shifting export flows from Asia as supportive factors.
Ardmore also expects depleted petroleum product inventories to provide another layer of tanker demand as refiners and importers rebuild stocks, estimating that the decline in global product inventories represents more than 600 MR-equivalent cargoes.
Beyond immediate inventory and seasonal factors, Ardmore sees structural changes in the global refining system increasing the shipping intensity of petroleum trade. Refinery closures in Western markets are increasingly being replaced by new capacity in the East, widening the geographic distance between where fuels are produced and where they are consumed.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Shu ling Lee

ICE front-month Singapore 10 ppm gasoil swap open interest hits near-nine-year-low in Oct
Oct open interest falls 10.3% MOM to 23.477 million barrels
Open interest for Middle East gasoil futures up 9.10% MOM
The open interest for front-month November Singapore 10 parts per million sulfur gasoil swap contracts traded on the Intercontinental Exchange fell 10.33% month over month to 23.477 million barrels in October, the latest ICE data showed Nov. 4.
Front-month open interest for the contract fell to a near nineyear low in October, and was last recorded lower at 18.613 million barrels in January 2018.
The fall in open interest came in line with bearish market sentiment, with South Korea having eased its diesel export cap restrictions to 110% of volumes in the corresponding month a year prior, from the existing cap of 100%, while Chinese cargoes had flooded the market after the government issued its fourth batch of clean oil product quotas of 5.65 million mt.
The Platts-assessed FOB Singapore 10 ppm sulfur gasoil frontmonth derivative time spread -- an indicator of near-term market outlook -- averaged $3.58/b in October, plunging $3.43/b from $7.01/b in September, Platts data showed.
Front-month open interest for the Singapore Gasoil 10 ppm/ ICE Gasoil spread rose 8.76% month over month to 29.236 million barrels in October, while open interest for the Singapore Gasoil 10 ppm/Dubai increased 14.40% to 2.988 million barrels over the same period, ICE data showed. Both spreads have registered open interest for a seventh consecutive month. Prior to this, no front-month open interest had been recorded for the Singapore Gasoil 10 ppm/ICE Gasoil spread since November 2018, and for the Singapore Gasoil 10 ppm/Dubai spread since January 2020.
Refining margins narrowed amid the longer supply outlook, with the Platts-assessed front-month Singapore gasoil swap crack against the front-month Dubai swap averaging $63.97/b

in October, down from $74.38/b in September, and down from $67.42/b in August.
Open interest for front-month November FOB Arab Gulf gasoil swap contracts traded on ICE rose 9.10% month over month to 2.661 million barrels in October, rebounding from a four-month low, the exchange data showed Nov. 4.
The mid-term view had also weakened month over month. The Platts-assessed front-quarter Q1/Q2 Asian 10 ppm sulfur gasoil swaps time spread averaged plus $12.72/b over October, narrowing from an average spread of plus $17.39/b the previous month.

South Korea's S-Oil sells 300,000 b gasoil for Nov. 26-30 loading
India's east coast refineries run normally post cyclone Murasu

The Asian ultra-low sulfur gasoil market remained rangebound on Nov. 4, amid expectations that gasoil exports from China would remain unchanged from initial estimates.
Brokers pegged the balance-month November-December Singapore gasoil swaps time spread at $1.52/b in midafternoon Asian trading Nov. 4, compared with $1.42/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Nov. 3.
Chinese refiners will likely increase their total clean oil product exports to about 4 million metric tons in November after being awarded new quotas, from prior estimates of 3.13 million mt, Platts earlier reported.
However, gasoil exports would likely remain unchanged from the initial export target of about 800,000 mt for November.
Elsewhere, the resumption of Ukrainian drone attacks on Russian refineries since Oct. 30 have helped support crack spreads.
The Platts FOB Singapore 10 ppm sulfur gasoil front-month swap crack spread against the front-month Dubai swap--a measure of the product's relative strength to the crude from which it was refined--rose to $61.85/b on Nov. 3, compared with $57.71/b the day prior.
Russian diesel and gasoil exports rose in the week ended Oct. 29 to 416,000 mt, up from 306,000 mt the previous week, though the Ukrainian attacks may reverse the increase in exports in the coming weeks, Platts previously reported.

Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
ICE LSGO drops $67.75/mt on US election results
Ukrainian strikes take 47% Russian refining offline
European diesel prices plummeted Nov. 4, alongside the rest of the oil complex, after sweeping Democratic Party victories in the US reduced the risk of US-Iran escalation, according to some market watchers.
Platts assessed the front- month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract down $67.75/metric ton to $1,355.25/mt, mirroring bearish movements in crude futures on the day.
"This war must end, and make no mistake: whenever Trump defies the will of the American people or the law, we will launch investigations, take him to court, and entangle him in legislative razor wire," Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer said Nov. 4. "The days of Trump's supplicant Senate are over."
Beginning in 2027, Democrats will control both the US Senate and the House of Representatives for the first time since 2023, following a wave of victories in US elections on Nov. 3.
"A Democratic sweep changes the political cost of continuing the Iran war," Rystad Energy Chief Economist Claudio Galimberti said in a Nov. 4 note. "With both chambers now capable of passing resolutions, holding back war funding and delaying confirmations, the administration faces a shorter political runway and greater institutional pressure to find a Hormuz deal."
Despite easing sentiment over US-Iran relations, Russia-Ukraine hostilities pose a persistent upside risk to the diesel market.
"The massive toll taken on Russian refining by Ukrainian drone strikes over the past few days is bullish for global diesel cracks," said S&P Global Energy CERA analyst James Bambino. "We estimate nearly 47% of Russia's more than 7 million b/d of crude and condensate refining is now offline, with nearly 17%-19% taken offline in the last few days alone."
Russian diesel/gasoil exports to the Mediterranean totaled 240,000 mt in October, down 1.06 million mt, or 81.5%, from the same month in 2025, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.

Curtailed exports from Russia have created sustained tightness in the European and global diesel market.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sophia Aung
Marine Fuel Singapore's bunker sales seen at nine-month high in October: CAS Bunker fuel sales up 11.9% MOM at 5.23 million mt Bulk carriers lead with 3,134 bunkering events HSFO premium hits record $83.26/mt average in October
Singapore's bunker fuel sales are estimated to have risen 11.9% month over month to 5.23 million metric tons in October, the highest level since January, as bunkering activity and aggregate receiving tonnage increased sharply, according to the latest S&P Global Commodities at Sea nowcast.
The surge in demand came as the world's largest bunker market faced tight availability of prompt fuel. Delivered high sulfur fuel oil premiums reached a record monthly high in October, while the front-month low sulfur fuel oil crack recently climbed to its strongest level in more than four years.
October sales were also estimated to be up 9.9% year over year, CAS said in its Nov. 2 report.
The October estimate reflected a 12.5% month-over-month increase in aggregate bunker-receiver tonnage and an 8% rise in bunkering events. CAS identified 10,167 bunkering events in October, up from 9,411 in September and marking the highest monthly total since January 2024.
Bulk carriers accounted for the largest share, with 3,134 bunkering events, up from 2,883 in September. Chemical/product tankers followed with 1,579 events, up from 1,470 a month earlier, while crude tankers recorded 1,047, up from 857. Container ships accounted for 981 events, and product tankers for 953.
CAS estimated October HSFO sales at 2.17 million mt, LSFO sales at 2.63 million mt and marine gasoil sales at 437,000 mt, representing market shares of 41.4%, 50.2% and 8.4%, respectively. The balance between high- and low-sulfur fuels

Fujairah November ex-wharf premiums dip MOM amid lackluster demand
Ex-wharf buyers cautious Kuwaiti LSFO shipments boost supplies Delivered LSFO bunker premium falls 15%

Supply crunch shadows demand rebound The stronger bunker demand came as buyers faced tight prompt
availability, with Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, reporting a significant supply crunch in the first half of October, when most LSFO and HSFO suppliers could offer deliveries only after mid-October.
The tightness was particularly pronounced in HSFO. The delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over the FOB Singapore benchmark averaged $83.26/mt in October, the highest monthly average on record.
The previous monthly high was $63.86/mt in March, based on Platts assessments. Meanwhile, the average Singapore-delivered LSFO bunker premium rose 23% to $76.01/mt in October from $61.80/mt in September.
Fuel oil cracks strengthen Firmness was also evident further upstream in the fuel oil
complex, with cargo supply tightening amid reduced Middle Eastern inflows as high freight rates curbed cargo movements.
The Singapore front-month 0.5%S marine fuel crack against ICE Brent reached $30.50/barrel on Oct. 30, up 17% week over week and marking its strongest level since July 2022. LSFO cash differentials also strengthened toward the end of the month amid concerns about near-term supply and uneconomic arbitrage flows from Western markets.
HSFO refining margins also strengthened sharply in October amid persistent supply tightness. The Singapore 380 CST HSFO crack rose about 196% during the month through Oct. 26 to $9.77/b, although market sources said elevated cracks were dampening refinery feedstock demand for the residual grade.
Singapore's commercial stocks of heavy distillates stood at 18.68 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 28, down 0.9% week over week and 24.6% year over year, according to Enterprise Singapore data.
-- Mia Pei, Max Lin

November ex-wharf premiums for low and high sulfur fuel oil cargoes at the Middle Eastern bunkering hub of Fujairah were stable to lower month over month, on the back of pressured downstream valuations, as tepid demand led to competition among suppliers.
However, cargo costs are still elevated as shipping out of the Gulf region remains a hassle due to on-and-off attacks around the Strait of Hormuz, according to at least three traders based in the UAE Nov. 4.
"Freight is through the roof," a trader said. Offer levels for balance-month November 0.5%S marine fuel ex-wharf cargoes retreated to premiums of $75-$80/metric ton over the benchmark Singapore Marine Fuel 0.5%S cargo values, compared with premiums of $80-$90/mt seen in October. Trades for November LSFO ex-wharf loadings were also heard at around mid-$70s/mt premiums, data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, showed. LSFO exports from Kuwait picked up in recent weeks, with the latest cargo of about 40,000 mt arriving at the port of Fujairah Nov. 2, according to the traders and data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea. Two physical bunker fuel suppliers at Fujairah have bought part of the cargo, one of the traders added. Prior to this latest shipment, 100,000 mt of Kuwaiti LSFO was also discharged at Fujairah Oct. 10, the traders said. Meanwhile, 380 CST HSFO ex-wharf barrels were offered at premiums ranging about $80/mt to $95$105/mt over the FOB Arab Gulf 180 CST HSFO cargo assessments, largely steady from October premiums, Platts data showed. "[Cargo suppliers are] offering but the bunker pool cannot digest [the price]," a trader said, with respect to the higher range of offers. The Fujairah bunker fuel market would quickly turn quiet if there was any escalation in conflict between the US and Iran, a trader said. As a result, physical bunker fuel suppliers were in a waitand-watch mode and were still reserved in procuring ex-wharf replenishments in larger volumes on a term basis, according to another trader. Platts assessed the Fujairah-delivered marine fuel 0.5%S bunker premium over the benchmark FOB Singapore cargo values at an average of $118.06/mt in October, down from the average of $138.80/mt in September. The Fujairah-delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over the 380 CST FOB Arab Gulf cargo also fell to an average of $128.34/mt in October, compared with $145.56/mt in September, Platts data showed.

ICE front-month Singapore HSFO open interest falls 19% MOM in October
Asian fuel oil supply concerns ease
Marine fuel front-month open interest up 15.11% MOM
The combined open interest for front-month Singapore high sulfur fuel oil contracts fell 18.80% month over month to 6.885 million metric tons as of Oct. 30, Intercontinental Exchange data showed Nov. 4.
The combined open interest includes contracts for the Singapore 380 CST and 180 CST HSFO grades, the Singapore viscosity spread and the Singapore 180 CST and 380 CST EastWest contracts.
Open interest for the front-month Singapore 380 CST contract fell 22.03% month over month to 4.161 million mt and open interest for 380 CST East-West contracts fell 36.68% month over month to 1.279 million mt, contributing to the decline in total front-month open interest.
Open interest across the HSFO forward curve at the end of October reached 33.574 million mt, rebounding from the four-year low of 33.447 million mt at the end of September.
The Asian HSFO market, which strengthened due to a persistent supply crunch in recent months, started to cool off amid growing expectations of increased supplies on hopes of easing Middle East conflict.
Although regular attacks on ships in the Strait of Hormuz alongside US military strikes on Iran keep flaring the supply concerns from the Middle East, trade sources said a potential peace deal could help reinstate some of the oil flows gradually.
Meanwhile, some Singapore-based trade sources said easing utility demand after the summer months could free up supplies and improve market fundamentals in the near term.

Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo's cash differential to the Mean of Platts Singapore 380 CST HSFO assessment at a premium of $32.50/ mt at the Nov. 3 Asian close, down about 35% day over day from $49.92/mt on Nov. 2, weighed by competitive offers from Chimbusco during the Platts Market on Close assessment process.
The benchmark HSFO cash differential is currently at its lowest level since Sept. 8, when it was assessed at a premium of $29.02/mt, Platts data showed.
The Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo price, assessed at $744.03/mt at the Nov. 3 Asian close, averaged $775.34/mt in October, compared with an average of $650.77/mt in September, Platts data showed.

Marine fuel oil The combined open interest for front-month Singapore marine
fuel oil contracts on ICE rose 15.11% month over month to 4.525 million mt on Oct. 30.
The combined open interest includes contracts for Singapore marine fuel oil 0.5%S, the marine fuel 0.5%S-380 CST HSFO spread -- called the Hi-5 spread -- and the marine fuel 0.5%S East-West contract.
The increase was led by a 23.34% rise in open interest for the marine fuel 0.5%S contract, which stood at 3.329 million mt.
Meanwhile, open interest for the marine fuel oil contracts across the forward curve rose 0.40% month over month to 23.833 million mt in October, largely stable from September.
The Asian LSFO market remains relatively buoyant amid tight finished-grade supplies, as arbitrage arrivals of on-specification materials and blending components from the western markets are expected to decline in November.
Singapore, the world's largest bunkering hub, is now expected to receive about 1.3-1.5 million mt of LSFO from the West in November, down from about 1.6-1.7 million mt in October.
Platts assessed the Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo's differential to the Mean of Platts Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S assessment at a premium of $29.08/mt at the Asian close Nov. 3, slipping from $31.79/mt on Nov. 2, weighed by a competitive offer from Glencore during the Platts physical trade window.
The Singapore 0.5% sulfur marine fuel cargo price, assessed at $831.36/mt on Nov. 3, averaged $857.99/mt in October, up from $801.47/mt in September, Platts data showed.

US DATA: Fuel Oil stocks rise on increased production, import levels
Inventories climb to 24.435 million barrels Production increases 28,000 b/d to 370,000 b/d Demand falls 90,000 b/d amid supply growth

US residual fuel oil stocks rose during the week ending Oct. 30, on increased production and import volumes, Energy Information Administration data released Nov. 4 showed.
US fuel oil stocks rose 1.036 million barrels to 24.435 million barrels on the week, the highest level observed since the week ending April 17, when stocks rose to 25.086 million. The US Gulf Coast accounted for the bulk of the increase, with levels climbing 487,000 barrels to 13.635 million, while US Atlantic Coast stocks increased 154,000 barrels to 5.351 million.
Total US import volumes also rose week over week, climbing by 71,000 barrels per day to 210,000 b/d. Imports into the USAC increased by 101,000 b/d to 176,000, while volumes into the USGC decreased 31,000 b/d to 30,000 during the same period.
Similarly, US fuel oil production increased on the week, rising by 28,000 b/d to 370,000 b/d. The US Gulf Coast recorded the largest gain, with production increasing by 14,000 b/d to 176,000 b/d, while production in the USAC climbed by 8,000 b/d to 41,000 b/d.
Total US domestic demand -- as measured by total product supplied - decreased on the week, as levels fell to an average of 282,000 b/d, down 90,000 b/d.
Total product supplied "approximately represents consumption of petroleum products" by way of quantifying "the disappearance of these products from primary sources," according to the EIA.
In the physical USGC HSFO market, Platts assessed Nov. 3 pricing down $9.89/b week on week to $75.06 /b.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy -- Humza Mirza

Platts Asia and Middle East Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
HSFO market structure inches down, crack plunges
380 CST HSFO cash premium drops to 8-week low
LSFO cash differential slips amid competitive offer
Asia's high-sulfur fuel oil market structure was seen inching lower Nov. 4, after refining margins and cash differentials for the residual fuel grade plunged amid growing expectations of increased supplies on hopes of an easing of the Middle East conflict.
The Singapore 380 CST HSFO balance NovemberDecember swap time spread was pegged at $46.75/metric ton in midafternoon Asian trading Nov. 4, compared with Platts' assessment of the spread at a backwardation of $47.25/mt at the Asian close Nov. 3.
This comes after Platts assessed the Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo's cash differential to the Mean of Platts Singapore 380 CST HSFO assessment at a premium of $32.50/mt at the Asian close Nov. 3, dropping nearly 35% day over day from $49.92/mt Nov. 2, weighed by competitive offers from Chimbusco during the Platts Market on Close assessment process.
The benchmark HSFO cash differential, which has shed 36.5% so far this week, was at its lowest level since Sept. 8, when it was assessed at a premium of $29.02/mt, Platts data showed.
Platts assessed the Singapore 180 CST HSFO cash premium over the MOPS 180 CST HSFO assessment at $40.92/mt at the Asian close Nov. 3, down $9/mt day over day, pressured lower by aggressive offers from Trafigura during the physical trade window.
Discussing the drastic decline in the HSFO fundamentals, one Singapore-based trader said, "all [in the market] are thinking peace deal [is coming soon]." Crude oil prices fell in midmorning Asian trade Nov. 4, extending losses for a third session, amid fresh diplomatic moves aimed at reducing the Middle East conflict.
Platts assessed the front-month Singapore 380 CST HSFO crack against prompt-month Brent crude, which has stayed in positive territory since late September, at $3.75/b on Nov. 3, down from $6.28/b in the preceding session, and its weakest since Oct. 5, when the HSFO refining margin was assessed at $1.97/b.
Although recent strength in HSFO margins has dampened feedstock demand from refiners, downstream bunker demand has remained relatively stable, according to trade sources.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over the FOB Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo assessment at $75.97/mt Nov. 3, up from $70.45/mt Nov. 2.

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
ARA market sees VLSFO loading delays
Gas prices boost Mediterranean LSFO demand
VLSFO loading delays tightened the market on Nov. 4, while LSFO competes with gas for power generation demand.
In the European VLSFO market, some participants saw supply tightness, partly stemming from loading delays at terminals within the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp region.
"Hearing a lot of issues with VLSFO," said a local ARA-based trader source
Other participants, however, describe supply-demand conditions as "balanced" on the week.
Within the 1% market, utility demand remains stable in the Mediterranean, supported by higher natural gas and LNG prices, which compete with fuel oil for power generation.
"I am seeing oil in the north," said a second trader source; however, elevated freight costs limit volumes moving on the arbitrage into the Mediterranean.
In the high sulfur fuel oil market, availability remained tight across both Northwest Europe and the Mediterranean, with participants pointing to reduced inflows from Latin America and continued blending constraints.
In the Mediterranean, a trader said fewer Venezuelan, Mexican and Colombian barrels had moved into Europe recently, while the cost and availability of cutter stocks continued to restrict production of finished RMG-grade material.
"There's oil on offer for sure, issue is RMG finished grade," the trader said.
Similar fundamentals were seen in Northwest Europe, where Rotterdam bunker suppliers reported "below-par" HSFO availability amid a lack of incoming product.
The same trader attributed the tightness across both regions to "the same rationale," with reduced Latin American flows limiting RMG product.
On the demand side, however, Rotterdam bunker activity remained subdued amid wider market volatility, limiting some of the impact of the tighter physical supply.
There were no trades in the MOC for fuel oil cargoes. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
-- Tommy Petrou

Feedstocks
Med, North Africa naphtha exports rise 8% in October as flows to Singapore surge
Algeria leads regional exports at 9.9 million barrels
Singapore imports surge to record 2.7 million tons
Mediterranean and North African naphtha exports rose 8% month over month in October to 19 million metric tons, with a sharp increase in flows to Singapore and China, S&P Global Commodities at Sea data showed.
Singapore emerged as a major outlet for Mediterranean and North African naphtha in October, with flows surging to a record high of 2.7 million mt, up sharply from just 85,100 mt in September, according to CAS data. The jump made Singapore the second-largest destination for Mediterranean and North African naphtha in October, narrowly behind the Netherlands, which received 2.8 million mt.
Shipments to China also climbed to 2.1 million mt from 804,900 mt in September, the highest since December 2025, when they stood at 2.1 million mt.
Combined exports from the Mediterranean and North Africa reached 19 million mt in October, up 8% from 17.6 million mt in September and marginally above the 18.9 million mt recorded in October 2025.
The month-over-month increase was almost entirely driven by North Africa, where loadings climbed to 11.8 million mt from 10.0 million mt in September, up 18%. Mediterranean exports eased slightly over the same period to 7.2 million mt from 7.6 million mt.
Algeria remained the largest source of naphtha, exporting 9.9 million mt in October, up from 8 million mt in September and accounting for just over half of combined Mediterranean and North African flows.
Italy was the second-largest exporter, with loadings rising sharply to 4.4 million mt from 2.9 million mt the previous month. Together, Algeria and Italy accounted for around three-quarters of the region's total October exports.
Elevated East-West naphtha spreads have supported the arbitrage from the Mediterranean into Asia, improving the economics of moving barrels east.
"Asian replacement buying, constrained Middle Eastern supply and expensive freight continue to support prompt structure and pull Atlantic barrels east," analysts at S&P Global Energy CERA said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- at $72.50/mt on Nov. 3, substantially above the Nov. 3, 2025 level of $34.25/mt.
The disruption at Hormuz has reset the economics of the naphtha trade, forcing Asia to compete more aggressively for alternative supply and keeping East-West spreads structurally elevated.
-- Barbara Fernandez-pita

ICE front-month CFR Japan naphtha swap open interest rises 5.20% MOM in Oct
Open interest for Month 1 to Month 13 contracts up 6.96% MOM
Downstream margins decline MOM
The open interest for front-month November CFR Japan naphtha swap contracts on the Intercontinental Exchange rose 5.20% month over month to 2.183 million metric tons in October, ICE data showed Nov. 3.
Front-month open interest volumes rebounded from a near two-year low, and it was last recorded lower at 1.808 million mt in January 2025, the data showed.
Open interest further along the derivatives curve rose 6.96% month over month to 11.399 million mt in October for front-month to 13th-month forward CFR Japan naphtha swap contracts, the data showed.
The total volume of front-month to 13th-month contracts traded over the same period fell 34.44% month over month to 832,000 mt, according to the data.
Platts assessed the CFR Japan naphtha physical crack spread against front-month Brent futures up $46.41/mt month over month at an average of $185.07/mt in October, compared with $138.66/mt in September.
Asian naphtha strengthened in early October as high freight costs limited cargoes moving from the West of Suez, while Middle East shipping risks raised supply concerns. This lifted the CFR Japan naphtha crack spread to a near three-month high of $190.18/mt Oct. 8.
Freight pressure and tight supply continued through midmonth, supporting premiums for December-delivery cargoes.
By Oct. 30, C+F Japan naphtha cargo was assessed at a sixmonth high of $1,082.25/mt, driven largely by the spike in Brent crude benchmarks, while its cash differential rose to $57/mt, Platts data showed.
The front-month East-West spread -- the premium of the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap over the CIF NWE equivalent -- was assessed at $74.57/mt on average in October, up 25.42%, or $15.11/mt wider, than September's average at $59.45/mt.
Downstream, olefin margins deteriorated. The Platts-assessed CFR Northeast Asia ethylene-CFR Japan naphtha physical spread -- closely watched by petrochemical producers -- averaged $179.84/mt in October, down $97.01/mt month over month, falling below the typical breakeven spread of $250/mt for integrated producers, and below $300-$350/mt for non-integrated producers.
Higher feedstock costs continued to strain the margins of naphtha-based petrochemical producers despite rising polyethylene prices.
Platts assessed the October average of the Asian PXnaphtha spread at $315.17/mt, narrowing 3.24%, or $10.56/mt, from $325.73/mt in September, and punching above the typical breakeven level of around $280-$300/mt.

The key Singapore front-month reforming spread -- the difference between Singapore 92 RON gasoline and Singapore naphtha derivative, which is of particular interest to gasoline blenders, narrowed $6.95/b month over month, or 22.23%, to an average of $24.33/b in October, Platts data showed.
Mid-term view softened month over month. The Plattsassessed front-quarter Q1/Q2 CFR Japan naphtha swaps time spread narrowed $4.24/mt month over month to an average of plus $76.26/mt over October, while the Q2/Q3 spread narrowed by a larger magnitude at $13.95/mt to $49.97/mt over the same period.

Front-month open interest volumes rebounded from a near two-year low and were last recorded lower at 1.808 million mt in January 2025, the data showed.

Separately, Thailand's Rayong Olefins Co. is operating its naphtha-fed steam cracker in Rayong at around 85% in November, a company source said.

SCGC's Map Ta Phut Olefins Co. cracker, also in Rayong, was shut Nov. 1 for scheduled maintenance, Platts reported Nov. 2.
The initial plan was to ramp up operations to full capacity as Map Ta Phut Olefins Co goes offline, but with current high feedstock naphtha prices, plans to increase the rate have been shelved, the company source added.

ICE front-month CFR North Asia LPG swaps open interest falls 6% in October

The front-month CFR North Asia propane swap averaged $943.91/mt in October, up 13.51% from $831.55/ mt in September, according to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
In October, LPG markets in Asia and the Middle East were dominated by tight supply as conflict-related disruptions to shipping through the Strait of Hormuz and Bab al-Mandab Strait restricted cargo flows from the region.
CFR North Asia propane prices reached a seven-month high of $1,115.50/mt on Oct. 30, while the butane premium over propane widened to its highest level in more than four months. Strong seasonal demand for winter gasoline blending, combined with disruptions to Middle Eastern supply, further tightened butane availability.
However, underlying petrochemical demand remained subdued, with Chinese propane dehydrogenation plant operating rates near minimum economic levels at about 66%, limiting the recovery in buying interest despite seasonal winter restocking.
Meanwhile, open interest in front-month November Saudi CP propane swaps traded on ICE fell 8.72% month over month to 900,000 mt in October, ICE data showed.

China's LPG imports edge lower in October; US market share rises: CAS
US supplies 54% of China's October LPG Iranian shipments rise nearly 50% month over month Middle East-origin volumes increase 54% to 531,000 mt

China's LPG imports fell 0.77% month over month to 2.312 million metric tons in October, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data as of Nov. 4.
October imports were down 21.39% year over year, the CAS data showed.
The US remained China's largest LPG supplier in October, shipping 1.248 million mt, up 13.04% from 1.104 million mt in September. US cargoes accounted for 54.0% of China's total LPG imports, compared with 47.4% a month earlier.
Iranian LPG shipments to China rose 49.69% month over month to 241,000 mt in October. Iran accounted for 10.4% of China's total LPG imports, up from 6.9% in September, according to the CAS data.
Canada shipped 116,000 mt of LPG to China in October, down 58.72% from 281,000 mt in September. Its share of China's total imports fell to 5.0% from 12.1% the previous month.
Middle East-origin LPG volumes totaled 531,000 mt in October, up 54.4% from 344,000 mt in September, the CAS data showed.
Propane accounted for 1.811 million mt of China's October LPG imports, down 11.14% from 2.038 million mt in September. Butane imports rose 71.58% to 501,000 mt from 292,000 mt.
Propane represented 78.3% of China's total LPG imports in October, down from 87.5% in September, while butane's share increased to 21.7% from 12.5%, the CAS data showed.
In the second half of October, China's propane dehydrogenation plant operating rates edged higher as units restarted from maintenance, but tight imported LPG availability continued to constrain a broader recovery in run rates, Platts reported earlier.
PDH plant utilization rose to 66.33% as of Oct. 26, from 65.49% a week earlier, according to data from local energy information provider JLC. The rate, however, remained below the September average of 71.39%, suggesting October utilization was likely to remain below the previous month's level.
Operating rates could recover further, with plants totaling 1.35 million mt/year of PDH capacity scheduled to restart following

maintenance, potentially providing additional support to China's demand for imported propane. Despite the planned restarts, limited arrivals of imported LPG could cap plant utilization, market participants said.
Earlier, China's combined LPG imports from the UAE, Oman, Qatar, Kuwait and Saudi Arabia fell 48.8% year over year to 6.86 million mt over January-September, data from the General Administration of Customs showed. The five suppliers accounted for 32.4% of China's LPG imports over the period, down from 49.5% a year earlier, with volumes from each supplier declining.
The margin for converting propane into propylene was at 201 yuan/mt as of Oct. 25, compared with an average loss of 36 yuan/ mt in September, according to JLC data.
China's Zhenhua Petrochemical has delayed the restart of its 750,000-mt/year propane dehydrogenation plant in Shandong province, Platts reported Oct. 30. It was not immediately clear when the plant, which was shut down for scheduled maintenance Sept. 17, would be restarted, according to the Platts report.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed CFR North Asia propane cargoes at an average of $974.47/mt in October, up from $872.89/mt in September. CFR North Asia butane cargoes averaged $1048.19/mt in October, up from $906.84/mt in September.
Platts assessed FOB Arab Gulf propane cargoes at an average of $709.32/mt in October, up from $658.55/mt in September. FOB Arab Gulf butane cargoes averaged $761.34/mt in October, up from $698.68/mt in September.
-- Jeff Ong
US DATA: Propane, propylene stocks draw below expectations, exports rise
Inventories fall over 1.5 million barrels to 104.108 million barrels
Exports drop 364,000 b/d to below 1.9 million b/d
US Gulf Coast propane price heard significantly lower day on day
US propane and propylene inventories fell to their lowest since July during the week ended Oct. 30, above analyst projections as overall demand beat expectations, according to US Energy Information Administration supply and demand data published Nov. 4.
Stocks tightened by 1.627 million barrels to 104.108 million barrels, their lowest level since late July, when stocks were at 103.103 million barrels. The decline marks the fifth consecutive weekly stock draw, the first four-week span of inventory tightening since late December and into early January.
For the first time this year, stock levels fell below year-ago levels of 106.094 million barrels for the week ended Oct. 31, 2025, down to 104.108 million barrels.
The US Gulf Coast accounted for about half the entire stock draw, with regional stocks down 872,000 barrels to 64.684 million barrels.
Total demand, including exports and domestic product supplied, rose 316,000 barrels/day to 3.309 million b/d, according

November 4, 2026

to EIA data. Of that total, exports fell 364,000 b/d to 1.869 million b/d while domestic product supplied made up the difference, up 680,000 b/d to 1.44 million b/d.
The stock draw was higher than expected by S&P Global Energy CERA analysts, who projected a 1.061-million-barrel draw, with expectations that inventories would fall below levels seen at the same time the year prior and exports would dip to 2.15 million b/d.
"Demand is projected to rise by 200,000 b/d from the previous week to 3.2 million b/d on the back of robust exports and peak crop drying demand," CERA analysts said in their Nov. 2 propane market forecast.
S&P Global Commodities at Sea shipping data, meanwhile, showed a week-over-week export increase, to 2.1 million b/d for the week ended Oct. 30, versus the 1.869 million b/d in the EIA report for the same period.
Production volumes rose slightly to 2.894 million b/d, holding fairly steady over the last two months. Propane and propylene production has averaged 2.931 million b/d since the first week of September.

Platts Asian LPG Daily Commentary
North Asia LPG prices drop for third consecutive day
China's October LPG imports fall 21% YOY
US supplies 54% of China's LPG needs
CFR North Asia LPG prices fell for a third consecutive session at the Nov. 4 Asian close, pressured by a $25.20/metric ton decline in front-month Brent crude futures, following signals of progress in US-Iran diplomacy amid the US midterm elections.
Paper market activity remained slower as participants continued to assess the US midterm election results.
Platts assessed CFR North Asia propane and butane cargoes at $1,059.50/mt and $1,149.50/mt, respectively, on Nov. 4, both down $21/mt from the previous session.
China's LPG imports fell 0.77% month over month to 2.312 million mt in October, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data as of Nov. 4. October imports were down 21.39% year over year, the CAS data showed.
The US remained China's largest LPG supplier in October, shipping 1.248 million mt, up 13.04% from 1.104 million mt in September. US cargoes accounted for 54% of China's total LPG imports, compared with 47.4% a month earlier, CAS data showed.
Platts assessed CFR East China propane at $1,068/mt on Nov. 4, down $16/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed CFR South China propane at $1,064/mt on Nov. 4, down $16/mt day over day.
Four bids were heard during the trading session Nov. 4, but no trades were concluded at the market close.

Total open interest in front-month November CFR North Asia LPG swaps traded on the Intercontinental Exchange fell 5.53% month over month to 2.48 million mt in October, the latest ICE data showed Nov. 3.
Open interest in front-month November CFR North Asia propane swaps traded on ICE fell 6.28% month over month to 2.433 million mt in October. Open interest in front-month November CFR North Asia butane swaps traded on ICE rose 72% month over month to a three-month high of 43,000 mt in October. It was last higher in August at 52,000 mt, the data showed.
In chemical news, Singapore's PCS plans to shut its No. 2 naphtha-fed steam cracker and its connecting butadiene extraction unit for maintenance from the middle of July to midto late August of 2027, Platts reported Nov. 4, adding that the butadiene unit would be shut for a few days longer than the steam cracker.
The steam cracker can produce 625,000 mt/year of ethylene and 320,000 mt/year of propylene, while the butadiene unit can produce 100,000 mt/year.

Platts European Propane Daily Commentary
Rhine water levels constrain barge capacity
Polish crop drying drives propane demand
CIF large cargo prices drop $43.50/mt daily
Northwest European propane availability remained constrained Nov. 4, as low Rhine levels disrupted inland deliveries and crop-drying demand supported Polish requirements ahead of a broader pickup in winter heating consumption.
Spot barge offers remained scarce, with contract liftings dominating activity. Low water levels restricted barge loads to around half capacity, limiting the volumes buyers could receive and their ability to accommodate additional spot purchases.
One trader described the reduced deliveries as "a nightmare," with recent loads falling below half the usual tonnage.
October barge loadings had been capped at around 50% capacity, while rail transport was being maximised to compensate for the shortfall.
An improvement in Rhine levels could allow larger barge loads toward the end of November, although this remained an unconfirmed expectation.
Any recovery would help ease inland transport constraints, but full loading capacity was not assured.
In Poland, crop drying supported propane consumption, while mild temperatures kept heating demand subdued. Temperatures of around 10 degrees Celsius had delayed a significant increase in residential requirements, leaving agricultural demand as a more immediate driver of buying interest.
"The whole complex is short at the moment," another source said, pointing to widespread propane tightness.
The prospect of stronger heating demand added uncertainty to the outlook.
With availability already constrained before winter consumption gathered pace, a sustained fall in temperatures

could intensify competition for prompt supply across Northwest Europe and Poland.
In the propane CIF NWE large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $807.50/metric ton, down $43.50/mt day over day, at a $16.50/ mt premium over the propane CIF November swap, $2.50/mt lower than premium levels.
In the propane coaster market, no bids, offers or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the propane coaster market at $898/mt, down $43/mt day over day, at a $90.50/mt premium to large cargoes.
In the propane barge market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the FOB Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp barges up $10.50/mt day over day at plus $75.50/mt versus the CIF large propane market, while FCA ARA market premiums to CIF large were at plus $90.50/mt.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu
Platts European Butane Daily Commentary
NWE butane supply tightens severely
Prices drop $33-$39/mt despite scarcity
US export disruptions worsen shortages
The Northwest European butane market remained tightly supplied Nov. 4, as depleted regional availability and limited replacement volumes from the US left buyers struggling to secure prompt cargoes.
"We're very much in a seller's market," a trader said, highlighting the scarcity of physical material across the region.
Availability from some UK supply points had been exhausted, with no further volumes expected until December.
Other established outlets had also seen supply dry up, leaving the market for 4,000-metric-ton butane parcels particularly constrained.
The shortage limited suppliers' ability to offer spot cargoes, with a lack of physical volume preventing firm offers in some cases.
US supply tightness and export disruptions further limited the ability of imports to offset regional production shortfalls, according to the trader.
The combination of restricted domestic availability and limited transatlantic replenishment left buyers with few alternatives to secure additional tons.
Prevailing buying indications remained insufficient to attract sellers, reflecting the limited availability of uncommitted material.

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
West Med LPG supply stays tight on scarcity
Butane drops $44/mt to $1,190 FOB assessment
North Africa demand drives regional competition
The West Mediterranean LPG market remained constrained Nov. 4, as limited regional availability and reduced access to replacement cargoes kept the supply outlook tight, particularly for butane.
Scarcity in Northwest Europe restricted the scope for additional coaster shipments into the Mediterranean. Limited availability at established export outlets left buyers with fewer alternatives, while US supply disruptions further reduced the potential for transatlantic cargoes to ease regional shortages.
Seasonal bottling requirements across North Africa and the eastern Mediterranean continued to support butane demand. These requirements maintained competition for available tons.
North African supply also faced buying interest from outside traditional Mediterranean destinations, limiting the volumes available for nearby outlets.
Turkish demand added to competition for Algerian and US cargoes, linking Mediterranean procurement more closely to wider Atlantic Basin availability.
Propane supply also remained tight, although heating demand remained temperature-dependent, with current conditions still described as mild. A broader seasonal pickup in consumption could place further pressure on availability as distributors sought to cover winter requirements.
The outlook remained dependent on import arrivals toward the end of November. Additional cargoes could provide some

relief heading into December, although the extent of any easing would depend on regional demand and the availability of further replacement supply.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,190/metric ton, down $44/mt day over day and stable as a percentage versus Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 136.75%, or at a $67/mt premium to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.
Platts assessed coaster freight from Lavera to Mohammedia at $72/mt, stable day over day, based on market information received throughout the day.
The CIF Mediterranean butane large cargo market was assessed at $930.75/mt, or 107% of naphtha.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB propane market at $990/mt, down $43.50/mt day over day.
The CIF Med propane large cargo market was assessed at $825.25/mt, or at a $17.75/mt premium to CIF Northwest Europe large cargoes.
In Morocco, Platts calculated the CIF very large cargo freight forward at $986.50/mt for delivery 45-60 days forward, down $41.75/mt.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu
Tankers
Hormuz transits dip to 19 on Nov. 3 as ship attacks persist
Eight vessels leave the Gulf through the strait
Iran claims strikes on multiple tankers
Vessel transits through the Strait of Hormuz edged down to 19 on Nov. 3 from 20 the previous day, S&P Global Commodities at Sea data showed, as continued tanker attacks in the strait kept crossings subdued.
Eight vessels left the Gulf through the strait while 11 entered, CAS reported on Nov. 4. Eight of the 19 crossings were observed as linked to Iran, while 11 were "dark" crossings, in which ships' AIS signals were interrupted or only partly visible.
Crude tankers made four outbound and three inbound transits over the day, according to CAS. The outbound ships included the VLCCs New Dynasty and Torkel, both laden with Basrah Medium crude, CAS reported. Sinokor's Germany B and the Suezmax Gol were also observed to have exited on Nov. 3 as both reappeared with AIS signals in the Gulf of Oman that day.
Among inbound VLCCs were the Sinokor-operated Suriname Prosperity and Spain B tankers, as well as ADNOC's Al Semaih, which CAS was unable to track since Nov. 2 when it was off Muscat.
Two LR1 tankers were also seen leaving the Gulf, according to CAS, including ADNOC's Mezaira'a, which reappeared off Fujairah and was assessed as potentially laden with clean petroleum products.

Vessel strikes continue As conflict negotiations continue, concerns over a flare-up
in aggression and volatility with shipping through the region are keeping Strait of Hormuz traffic low, all while strikes against commercial ships within the strait persisted on the day.
The Iranian Revolutionary Guard reported the destruction of three "violating oil tankers" in the Strait of Hormuz during the early hours of Nov. 3, while four other oil carriers were reportedly destroyed following a "mine collision" later that day, the IRGC's news carrier Sepah News reported on Nov. 4.
The UK Maritime Trade Operations Centre reported only one vessel attack on Nov. 3 approximately 14 nautical miles northeast of Lima, Oman at 19:00 UTC. The tanker had been struck by two unknown projectiles that hit its engine room, according to the UKMTO incident report.
Security firm Vanguard TECH identified the impacted tanker as the Barbados-flagged oil tanker Grit, stating that it had been struck by two projectiles in Omani territorial waters. The tanker's status and the extent of damage remain unknown, Vanguard reported.
CAS showed the Grit oil tanker has had its AIS signal off for over a month, and was last observed off the coast of Ras Al Khaimah, UAE, on Sept. 28.
At the Red Sea, vessel traffic through the Bab al-Mandab Strait fell sharply to 30 crossings on Nov. 3 from 48 transits the day prior as fewer tankers crossed the strait on the day, CAS reported.
CAS reported Nov. 3 traffic through the Red Sea strait comprised eight tankers, 10 bulk carriers, five container ships and seven miscellaneous vessels, with 11 crossing northbound and 19 headed southbound.
-- Lulwa Albuainain, Aresu Eqbali

Torm expects strong product tanker rates to persist as disrupted Middle Eastern oil flows, reduced shipping capacity and an eventual inventory restocking cycle support tanker demand, the Danish company said Nov. 4 while reporting sharply higher third-quarter earnings.
While total exports of oil products from the Middle East have fallen during the US-Israel war with Iran, tanker rates are rising toward record levels as more ships are needed to mitigate logistics issues, with Gulf states transporting their cargoes via ship-to-ship transfers amid security concerns.
"Favorable market conditions, healthy transportation demand and strong execution contributed to a strong third quarter," CEO Jacob Meldgaard said. "We remain confident in the long-term outlook for the product tanker market."
Platts' Global Clean Tanker Index for scrubber-fitted, eco ships reached $146,688.69/day Nov. 3, slightly down from $158,256.96/ day Oct. 27, the highest since the assessment began in November 2025. The index's third-quarter average was $68,547.27/day, up from $56,503.66/day in the second quarter.
Even when Persian Gulf crude flows start to normalize, higher feedstock availability for refineries would lead to recovery in clean-product exports, Torm said in a quarterly report.
The company also anticipates inventories depleted during recent market disruption will eventually require replenishment, potentially adding seaborne volumes and ton-mile demand.
More than 60 VLCCs were employed in Persian Gulf shuttle operations by the start of the fourth quarter, while close to 20 Long Range (LR) 2 tankers were involved in shuttle movements of clean products, according to Torm's estimates.
Around two-thirds of the LR2 fleet remained in dirty trades during the third quarter, effectively reducing clean petroleum product carrying capacity by about 4% from the start of 2026, Torm said.
Strong earnings As of Oct. 28, Torm had covered 53% of fourth-quarter earning
days at an average of $78,419/day, including 68% of LR2 days at $112,150/day, 38% of LR1 days at $106,271/day and 49% of Medium Range (MR) days at $59,722/day.
The company consequently raised the lower end of its 2026 guidance by $50 million, forecasting time charter equivalent earnings of $1.75 billion-$1.90 billion and EBITDA of $1.35 billion-$1.50 billion.
TORM achieved fleet-wide TCE of $40,102/day in the third quarter, down 32.4% from $59,301/day in the second quarter but 29.3% above $31,012/day a year earlier. LR2 TCE averaged $52,575/ day, down 21.5% quarter over quarter but up 35.9% year over year.

LR1 earnings averaged $38,667/day and MR earnings $36,137/day, respectively, 31% and 26% above the third quarter of 2025.
Quarterly TCE earnings rose 49.6% year over year to $353 million, while EBITDA climbed 80.3% to $274 million. Net profit more than doubled to $199 million from $78 million, with revenue rising 45% to $499 million.
Fleet renewal Torm sold the 2007-built LR1 Torm Venture and 2008-built MR
Torm Laura during the third quarter, leaving an operating fleet of 95 vessels. The company had acquired six MR resales in the second quarter and ordered six MR newbuildings, subsequently exercising options for another two.
After quarter-end, Torm agreed to order eight LR2 newbuildings through a 50-50 joint venture, with deliveries expected in 2029-2030, and to secure options for two additional MRs. Newbuilding and secondhand-vessel commitments stood at $608.8 million as of Sept. 30.
"We are investing selectively in additional newbuildings to renew and grow our fleet," Meldgaard said.

News
Democrats take control of US Congress, gain leverage over Trump energy agenda
Democrats gain House and Senate majorities
Oversight targets include coal, mining, LNG policies
Democrats are poised to gain broad oversight powers over the Trump administration's energy policy agenda in the next Congress, with a projected House majority and control of a narrowly divided US Senate.
Nov. 3 midterm election returns show Democrats with a net gain of at least 13 seats in the US House of Representatives, putting the party on track to hold at least 227 seats in the 435-member chamber, with 69 races still undecided early Nov. 4.
Democrats are on track to control at least 54 seats in the US Senate after flipping seats previously held by Republicans in Iowa, Kansas, Maine, North Carolina, Ohio, South Carolina and Texas.
Democrats' final margin in the Senate was still undecided early Nov. 4 as Alaska and Mississippi continued to count votes in closely contested races. The party's final House margin could take longer to determine as California tallies votes in a potentially weekslong process.
Congressional oversight implications Democrats are on track to capture the House majority as
President Donald Trump enters the final two years of a term that has already seen massive swings in federal energy policy. The Trump administration has largely dismantled and replaced Bidenera energy and climate policies, focusing on national energy dominance and dispatchable power sources.
With control of key energy-related House committees, Democrats are set to gain subpoena power to investigate a host of Trump administration actions. Areas of focus are expected to include the administration's critical minerals agreements and equity stakes in mining companies; emergency actions delaying coal plant retirements; and consumer price impacts tied to the president's foreign policy decisions.
Beyond hearings and investigations, House Democrats are expected to advance affordability-related legislation, including measures to address cost allocation for large-load customers, such as data centers, in the run-up to the 2028 presidential election.
Even with control of both chambers, Democrats would have relatively few avenues to enact major energy policy changes in the face of opposition from congressional Republicans and Trump. The Senate's 60-vote filibuster threshold and the

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

41

November 4, 2026

president's veto power would pose formidable hurdles to legislation lacking significant bipartisan support.
Democrats could, however, use the annual appropriations process to press energy policy priorities and potentially attach policy riders to must-pass spending measures.
Democratic control of Congress would also deny Republicans access to the budget reconciliation process during the remainder of Trump's term. Both parties have used the process, which allows legislation to bypass the Senate's filibuster threshold, to enact sweeping energy-related changes to US tax and spending policy over the last two Congresses.

Democratic Senate complicates Trump nominations Senate Democrats are set to gain new powers to constrain
Trump's personnel picks, including cabinet-level nominees, federal judges and nominees to independent agencies.
The shift could carry significant implications for federal energy policy. Senate Democrats are poised to control the confirmation process for nominees to agencies, including the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission, where turnover in the next Congress could affect federal policy on electricity markets, transmission and natural gas infrastructure.
A Democratic Senate could also complicate any potential effort by Trump to fill a Supreme Court vacancy. The court's 6-3 conservative majority, featuring three Trump nominees, has already curtailed judicial deference to federal agencies and expanded presidential control over traditionally independent agencies.
Democratic control of both chambers would put a premium on bipartisan dealmaking for legislation capable of clearing Congress and earning Trump's signature. Congressional observers still see

Nigeria's Dangote launches Namibian fuel storage and pipeline project
Project cost to hit $2.5B, Dangote tells launch
1,700 km pipeline to serve six African nations
Dangote receives Egyptian delegation in Lekki
Dangote, the owner of Nigeria's huge 700,000 barrel/day Dangote refinery, broke ground on a 4-million-barrel petroleum storage facility and regional pipeline project in Namibia on Nov. 4, with a ceremony in Walvis Bay.
Addressing the event in the presence of Namibian president Netumbo Nandi-Ndaitwah, Aliko Dangote, Africa's richest man, said the tank farm, located 10 km east of Walvis Bay in western Namibia, would have the capacity to store 600 million liters of refined products.
It will feature a 360-million-liter diesel pipeline and a 240-million-liter gasoline pipeline, Dangote said, along with truck loading, dispensing and associated infrastructure.
The 1,700-kilometer pipeline system will be part of a "broader distribution network serving Botswana, Zambia, Zimbabwe, Malawi and southern DRC," Dangote said, "establishing Walvis Bay as a strategic Atlantic energy gateway" and an "integrated supply corridor linking the coast to the hinterland."
The final route will be determined through engineering studies and regulatory approvals, the CEO said, adding that the project investment is expected to reach about $2.5 billion.
He added that the "historic occasion" would strengthen Namibia's energy security and advance regional trade and industrial development in Southern Africa and establish a platform for "regional commerce and shared prosperity."
Nandi-Ndaitwah said the project would ensure "supply security for Namibia and its landlocked neighboring countries," adding that she had opted to place the project "directly into the office of the president."
Separately, on the upstream side, Namibia is currently holding negotiations with oil companies including TotalEnergies following huge crude discoveries in its offshore Orange Basin. Officials estimate the country holds 11 billion barrels of oil resources and hope to see first oil in around 2030.
African countries, even those that produce oil, tend to be heavily dependent on fuel imports, with minimal refining capacity on the continent, making them vulnerable to shocks like fallout from the US-Iran war.
Dangote's plant outside Lagos, which came online in 2024, has revamped West Africa's fuel sector, and the company is planning to build another 700,000 b/d refinery in Kenya's eastern port town of Lamu.
The company has also inked fuel supply and pipeline deals with numerous countries across the continent in recent months.

ICE front-month MOPAG oil products open interest rises 11% in October
Gasoil open interest rises 9.1% on month
Jet open interest nearly doubles in October
Naphtha open interest rises on month

The rise in Arabian Gulf premiums had tracked the weakening differentials of Singapore-loaded cargoes, which narrowed to an average of $1.73/b from $3.96/b in September, due to limited progress in US-Iran peace talks and continued attacks on ships within the region, which had helped support prices in October, according to gasoil traders.
The front-month gasoil EFS -- the spread between Singapore 10 ppm sulfur gasoil swaps and the corresponding ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract -- averaged minus $154.91/mt in October, widening from minus $148.39/mt in September, data from Platts showed.
Market participants closely watch the gasoil EFS spread as an indicator of East-West arbitrage economics, and a steeply negative EFS spread typically indicates more lucrative East-West arbitrage flows.
India had supplied around 1.1 million metric tons of gasoil to Europe in October, while Saudi Arabia had supplied 284,000 mt over the same period, Platts previously reported.
Prices of ultra-low sulfur gasoil barrels loaded within the Strait of Hormuz were also on the rise amid increased shipping traffic through the vital key point, increasing 23.89% to an average of minus $14.19/b in October, from minus $18.65/b in September, Platts data showed.
The Platts FOB Fujairah gasoil cargo assessment averaged $173.63/b in October, easing from September's average at $177.56/b, but higher than August's average at $154.54/b.
Meanwhile, co-distillate jet fuel/kerosene derivatives nearly doubled in open interest for front-month November contracts in October, up 88.64% month over month to 83,000 barrels.
The Platts FOB Arab Gulf jet fuel/kerosene front-month swap crack spread versus the front-month Dubai swap -- a measure of the product's relative strength to the crude from which it was refined -- averaged $49.10/b in October, narrowing from $58.87/b in September.
The FOB Fujairah jet fuel/kerosene cargo price averaged $174.78/b in October, strengthening from an average of $173.55/b in September and $149.75/b in August.
Platts began publishing FOB Fujairah assessments for middle distillates on Oct. 3, 2016.

Total open interest for front-month November Arab Gulf middle distillate swaps contracts on the Intercontinental Exchange rose 10.51% month over month to 2.744 million barrels in October, according to the latest Intercontinental Exchange data.
Leading the increase, open interest for front-month November FOB Arab Gulf gasoil swap contracts rose 9.1% month over month to 2.661 million barrels in October, rebounding from a four-month low of 2.439 million barrels in September, according to the ICE data published on Nov. 3.
Cash differentials for FOB Arab Gulf 10 ppm sulfur gasoil cargoes rose $1.74/barrel month over month, or 9.44%, to an average of $20.15/b in October, from $18.41/b in September, data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, showed.

Naphtha interest more than doubles on month In light ends, open interest for the front-month November
MOPAG naphtha swaps more than doubled, rising 114.71% month over month to 73,000 mt, ICE data showed.
The Platts front-month FOB Arab Gulf naphtha swap averaged $732.89/mt in October, inching up 3.81% from $705.98/mt in September and $673.74/mt in August.
The FOB Fujairah naphtha cargo price averaged $805.37/mt in May, falling 6.23% from the previous month's average, Platts data showed.
Open interest for the front-month November MOPAG 92 RON gasoline swap remained at zero in October, according to ICE data.
The front-month FOB Arab Gulf 92 RON gasoline swap averaged $110.60/b in October, slipping 3.79% from September's average.

Hungary aims for the JANAF crude oil pipeline to be able to "continuously and fully supply both the Százhalombatta and Bratislava refineries" when it is needed, Orbán added.
Hungary's need for the JANAF pipeline capacity has significantly increased in recent years, after Russian crude supply to the EU came under significant restrictions in response to Russia's fullscale invasion of Ukraine. While most EU countries were banned from supplying Russian crude, Hungary has received an exception to continue to import crude through the Druzhba pipeline.
"The United States has granted Hungary an exemption until Nov. 21 of this year from the tariffs it may impose on countries that import Russian crude oil. This applies to crude oil purchased from Lukoil and Rosneft, and MOL is already working on creating the conditions for a technological transition at its refineries so that it can potentially source non-Russian crude oil from other sources in the south via JANAF," Orbán said.
