Hungary hopes to finalize NIS refinery sale to MOL by Dec.11
MOL pursues majority stake in Serbian refiner US sanctions waiver expires December 11 JANAF pipeline gains importance for Hungary

South Korea's exports on track to top $1 trillion as oil product values surge
Jan-Oct exports climb 93.7% to reach $930 billion Refining margins drive 80% oil product gain Seoul raises diesel export ceiling to 110%

Serbia, Hungary and Russia aim to finalize the sale of the Naftna Industrija Srbije refinery to the Hungarian company MOL by mid-December, Hungary's Foreign Minister Anita Orbán announced.
"According to the information I have, there is a strong intention on all sides -- Serbian, Russian and Hungarian -- to finalize this transaction by Dec. 11," Anita Orbán said in an interview to the Hungarian media outlet Telex on Nov. 3.
MOL signed an agreement with the Serbian government in mid-June for a majority stake in NIS, following an earlier agreement to purchase the entire 56.15% Russian stake held by Gazprom-affiliated branches of Serbia's refiner.
As Gazprom Neft, which owns a 44.85% stake in NIS, was sanctioned by the US in January 2025, the companies need to obtain final approval from the US Department of the Treasury to finalize the deal. A 11.3% stake in the Serbian refiner is held by JSC Intelligence, which is managed by Gazprom Capital.
"MOL's acquisition in Serbia -- the purchase of the Russian stake in NIS -- could also help Hungary's negotiations related to JANAF, as the Pancevo refinery is supplied via this Croatian pipeline," Orbán said.

South Korea's cumulative exports exceeded $900 billion for the first time in January-October, putting the country on course to surpass $1 trillion in exports for 2026, as refined oil product sales contribute to the milestone amid robust refining margins, according to the trade ministry and industry sources over Nov. 2-4.
The country would become only the fourth nation in the world to achieve annual exports of $1 trillion, with the threshold expected to be crossed in November, the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources said in a statement Nov. 2.
South Korea's exports rose 93.7% year on year to $115.28 billion in October, the second-highest monthly total on record after September's $121.05 billion. Exports for January-October reached $930 billion, leaving $70 billion needed in November to reach the annual milestone, according to MOTIR.
Semiconductors remained the leading export item and the main driver of growth. Semiconductor exports surged 259.4% year over year to $56.44 billion, supported by strong global demand for memory chips used in artificial-intelligence applications and elevated prices as major technology companies expanded data-center and other AI-related capital spending, MOTIR said.

Seoul allows more diesel exports Seoul recently raised the ceiling on diesel exports to 110%
of the corresponding month a year earlier, from 100%, provided refiners maintain domestic supplies and adequate inventories, Platts reported previously.
South Korea--Asia's top middle-distillate supplier--exported 14.9 million barrels of diesel in November 2025, meaning refiners could seek to sell close to 16.4 million barrels to overseas customers this month, according to the product managers at the Ulsan- and Seosan-based refiners and a sales trader at the Singapore-based trading office of another South Korean refiner, citing the latest data from state-run Korea National Oil Corp. issued Oct. 30.

The gains came as regional middle-distillate markets remained highly profitable, product managers at two major South Korean refiners based in Ulsan and Seosan told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, during market discussions over Nov. 2-4.
Platts assessed the second-month Singapore gasoil swap crack against Dubai crude at a record high of $83.02/b on Sept. 16. The crack spread averaged $72.97/b in September, compared with $64.71/b in August and the 2025 average of $18.16/b. Platts assessed the crack spread at $60.6/b on Nov. 3.
Platts also assessed the FOB Singapore second-month jet fuel/kerosene crack spread against Dubai swaps at an average of $50.14/b in 2026 to date, more than double the 2025 average of $17.32/b.

South Korea has about 3.4 million b/d of refining capacity, well above domestic petroleum demand of around 2.3 million b/d, leaving substantial capacity for exports when local supply conditions permit.
"The key is for refiners, and the government is to make sure adequate feedstock crude procurement continues to keep up the essential fuel production to meet both domestic and global customer demand," the middle distillate product manager at the Ulsan-based refiner told Platts Nov. 4.

Strong demand for diesel, jet fuel, kerosene and gasoline from markets including Australia, Japan, Europe, the US West Coast and other parts of Asia has supported South Korean refiners' export earnings, the Ulsan- and Seosan-based refinery product managers said.

IEA, IEF, OPEC discuss maritime energy security and market stability
Attacks targeting Middle East energy supplies
OPEC, IEA slash oil demand growth forecasts
Oil prices volatile amid conflict
OPEC, the International Energy Agency and the International Energy Forum held a technical workshop on maritime energy security and market stability Nov. 4, as conflict-related disruptions to energy supplies raise challenges for producers and consumers alike.
OPEC Secretary General Haitham al-Ghais said that maritime energy flows remain a core pillar of global energy security and participants in the OPEC+ agreement are fully committed to supporting the stability of global energy markets, according to statements released by OPEC and the IEF.
"In this regard, our discussions will be vital to help identify possible solutions to the challenges facing both producers and consumers in relation to current maritime trade," al-Ghais said, according to the statements.
Severe disruptions to shipping in the Middle East have shocked oil markets in 2026 since the US and Israel attacked Iran in February. This has led both OPEC and the IEA to slash their oil demand growth forecasts for 2026.
OPEC said Oct. 13 that it expects oil demand growth to be 200,000 barrels/day in 2026, down from its forecast of 1.4 million b/d before the conflict in the Middle East broke out.
The IEA said Oct. 14 that it expects world oil demand to fall by around 2.6 million b/d in 2026. Before the war, it was forecasting growth of around 850,000 b/d this year.
IEA Executive Director Fatih Birol said the group met to help find durable solutions to the "unprecedented" energy crisis, according to the statements.
"While producers have worked hard to maintain supply, under extremely difficult circumstances, we must all work to safeguard freedom of maritime navigation, a central pillar of global energy security," he said.

The IEA did not immediately respond to a request for comment on the talks.
IEF Secretary General Jassim Alshirawi said the group could strengthen the dialogue and data transparency needed to restore confidence, support investment and build more resilient global energy markets.
"Safe maritime passage is essential to energy security and market stability for producers, consumers and transit countries alike," he said.
Analysts at S&P Global Energy CERA see month-to-month oil supply volumes as unpredictable, but assume that exports will remain below prewar levels through 2027.
"A battle for airspace over the Arabian Peninsula and signs that the lull in fighting between the US, Israel and Iran may be ending have put oil infrastructure at risk, raising concerns about how reliably oil will keep flowing," CERA said.
The conflict has fueled oil price volatility in 2026. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Dated Brent at $144.42/b on April 7, up from $72/b before the war broke out. It assessed Dated Brent at $105.195/b on Nov. 3.
-- Rosemary Griffin
US firm Murphy Oil secures pair of oil blocks off Mauritania
PSCs signed for C10, Tiof offshore blocks
BP's GTA gas project dominates Mauritania energy sector
Mauritania has handed two offshore oil and gas blocks to US firm Murphy Oil, its energy ministry said in a statement late Nov. 3, as the West African country looks to build on its huge GTA gas project to expand its upstream sector.
The Ministry of Energy and Petroleum said Murphy -- which has assets in the Americas, Africa and Southeast Asia -- had signed production sharing contracts covering the C10 and Tiof offshore blocks in Mauritania's coastal sedimentary basin.
The deal was signed in the capital, Nouakchott, by energy minister Mohamed Abdel Vetah and Murphy's Senior Vice President of Exploration and Subsurface, Christopher Charles Olson.
"This landmark partnership with Murphy Oil Corporation marks a strategic turning point for Mauritania's hydrocarbons sector and further reinforces the country's position as an attractive and promising destination for investment in the oil and gas industry," the ministry said, adding that it reflects its competitive legal and regulatory framework, political stability and industry confidence.
"With its outstanding geostrategic advantages -- a highpotential offshore sedimentary basin, direct access to major global markets and a clear vision combining resource development with the energy transition -- Mauritania is positioning itself as a safe and competitive frontier for oil and gas exploration," it added.
Murphy did not immediately respond to a request for an official comment from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.

Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: Par Pacific sees wider crude differentials reshaping Pacific flows
Refineries: Four US refineries
Owner: Par Pacific
Overall capacity: 213,700 b/d
Units affected: Various
Duration: Q2-Q4 2026
Par Pacific's Hawaii refinery is running well after a plant-wide turnaround as wider crude differentials and higher tank freight

costs are reshaping crude and product flows in the Pacific, CEO Will Monteleone said on Nov. 4.
"The refined product market environment strengthened through the quarter and our combined market index averaged a quarterly record of $38.45/b, nearly three times the 2025 average of $12.40/b," he said on the third-quarter results call. "Constrained Persian Gulf and Russian refined product exports, restricted Asian refinery throughput and continued trade protectionism remain critical facts that limited refined product supply."
Demand remained resilient and global refined inventories remained tight, Monteleone added.
The company reported Q3 refining throughput of 174,000 barrels/day through its system. Hawaii ran at 57,000 b/d, held down by extended turnaround discovery work.
Par Pacific said the plant is now set for a five-year run and is benefiting from completed projects that support reliability and annualized capacity.
"We're encouraged by the rates that we're seeing out of Hawaii post turnaround," Monteleone said.
He said the refinery has at times topped 90,000 b/d, although he said it was too early to say that rate is consistent, adding that the company is working to run lighter crudes.
For Q4, Par Pacific guided Hawaii to 85,000-89,000 b/d and the whole system to a midpoint of 200,000 b/d.
Debottlenecking in Montana Par said its Billings, Montana, refinery set a quarterly record
of 58,400 b/d in Q3. Before Par Pacific bought the plant from ExxonMobil in 2022, the plant ran about 50,000 b/d at production costs above $12/barrel. Throughput has averaged 56,000 b/d year to date, and costs are now below $10/b.
Monteleone said Par Pacific is pursuing a number of debottlenecking projects, each costing less than $10 million, which could lift diesel or jet output by up to 1,000 b/d. Separate work in the alkylation units could debottleneck them by 10% to 20%.
Par's Washington and Montana run about 50,000 b/d of heavy crude, or 25% of system feedstock. And the widening heavy Canadian differentials by $15/b in the past month, helps offset the higher waterborne freight and wider Brent-West Texas Intermediate crude spreads, Shawn Flores, Par Pacific's CFO, said.
Wider crude differentials and higher freight costs Flores on the call guided Hawaii's fourth-quarter crude
differential at $9-$11/b, reflecting crude bought two to three months ahead, in early Q3.
Since then, delivered Alaska North Slope differentials to the US West Coast have widened by $10-$15/b, he said, referring to the US West Coast benchmark crude.
As ANS exports to Asia have increased, Q4 ANS differentials to WTI are averaging $24.19/b as of Nov. 4, compared with Q3 differentials of $6.27/b, while Q4 ANS differentials to ICE Brent are averaging $17.45/b as of Nov. 4, up from $1.57/b in Q3, according to Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.

November 4, 2026

ANS exports to Asia averaged 15,390 b/d in Q3, but as of Nov. 4, Q4 exports are almost double, averaging 36,760 b/d, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
As a result, CEO Monteleone said crude exporters far from their usual markets are discounting to clear barrels, and FOB differentials in some markets have swung from premiums of 3%5% to discounts of 5%-8%.
"Overall, Asian refinery runs remain constrained as the ability to run long-haul import-export refineries and an elevated freight rate environment faces pressure," Monteleone said, which will only increase in Asia and Europe as the winter heating season begins.
"The value of local manufacturing is going up in a more volatile world," he said.
-- Janet Mcgurty
REFINERY NEWS: Suncor streamlines turnarounds, embraces diesel, jet export markets
Refineries: Edmonton, Alberta; Sarnia, Ontario; Montreal, Quebec; and Commerce City, Colorado.
Owner: Suncor
Overall capacity: 146,000 b/d; 85,000 b/d; 137,000 b/d; and 103,000 b/d
Duration: Q3-Q4 2026
Suncor Energy ran its refineries at 95% utilization in the third quarter, despite turnarounds at two of its refineries and crude curtailment at another, CEO Rich Kruger said Nov. 4.
"Our third quarter involved major maintenance for the year, positioning for a strong finish, and maximizing value in today's business environment. We accomplished all three," he said on Suncor's third-quarter results call.
During the third quarter, Suncor had a 37-day turnaround at Edmonton, a 31-day event at Montreal and a curtailment at Sarnia tied to an unplanned Line 5 outage.
Suncor's third quarter refining throughput averaged 488,000 b/d or 95% of capacity, compared with 470,600 b/d in Q2.
"That left throughput about 28,000 b/d above its internal plan and level with last year's record, Kruger said. Suncor re-rated its downstream capacity to 511,000 b/d last year, and Kruger said the system has since run above 520,000 b/d on some days.
During the Edmonton turnaround on the gasoline-making fluid catalytic cracking unit, rather than reduce crude runs of between 45,000 -50,000 b/d, Suncor was able to store the heavier gas oil and blend it with the upgrader streams from its Fort McMurray oil production to create a new, sweet synthetic crude.
"This innovative idea enabled full crude throughput during the entire turnaround, preserving 46,000 b/d of throughput would have been curtailed, adding 3% to Edmonton's annual average utilization rate and 1% to Suncor's overall utilization rate," said Kruger, adding it created C$50 million in free funds.
Suncor said this raised custom-blend processing by 8,000 b/d this year, worth C$75 million with the added diesel yield bringing Edmonton's year-to-date gains to C$140 million.

Smaller gains at other refineries At Commerce City, Colorado, -- Suncor's only US refinery --
a fix to a Plant 3 furnace constraint lifted the unit to 43,000 b/d from a previous ceiling of 38,000 b/d. And the company is looking to do a similar project on Plant 2 furnace which will allow it to run new crudes which will help "fill up the bottom of crude pipe still," said Dave Oldreive, Suncor's head of refining, on the call.
At Sarnia, replacing an 800 b/d solvent stream let the reformers run at lower severity, increasing the yield and producing more high octane gasoline.
Oldreive said that cuts catalyst-change downtime and raises high-octane gasoline yield, worth about C$40 million a year.
"And I got some things going on in Montreal," he said, promising to provide more details next quarter.
Exports increase from both East and West coasts Suncor continues to export refined products both from its
Canadian West Coast Burrard terminal, where it has more than doubled its export capacity, and its Montreal, Quebec, refinery.
Between Burrard and Montreal, Suncor exported 92 cargoes over nine months, nearly 60% higher that total 2025 exports.
Jet sales during Q3 were 38,000 b/d, up 65% year over year and exports at 75,000 b/d are up 80% year over year, Oldreive said.
Earlier this year, Suncor completed a jet enhancement project at Montreal, which increased production by 18,000 b/d.
He said the company shifts between diesel and jet based on netbacks and exports very little gasoline. According to Commodities at Sea (CAS), Q3 exports of jet from Montreal averaged 6,770 b/d - heading to Northwest Europe and the US - while Montreal diesel exports averaged 2,450 b/d during the quarter destined for US New England states.
CAS data showed that Suncor's Burrard Products terminal near Vancouver, British Columbia, exported about 20,000 b/d of diesel to Latin America during Q3.
Oldrieve said that work rail loading at the facility - as past bottleneck -- has been addressed and leaves room for more exports in Q4 and Q1.
-- Janet Mcgurty

REFINERY NEWS: Cenovus downstream posts record Q3 earnings as Laurel opens up new markets
Refinery: Lloydminster Upgrader; Lloydminster refinery; Lima, Ohio; Toledo, Ohio; and Superior, Wisconsin.
Owner: Cenovus
Overall capacity: 78,500 b/d; 29,500 b/d; 170,000 b/d; 151,000 b/d; and 44,000 b/d.
Units affected: NA
Duration: Q3-Q4
Cenovus Energy's downstream business posted record earnings in the third quarter, propelled as strong refined product prices pushed benchmark cracks to record levels, Jon McKenzie said on Nov. 4.
"Strong financial product pricing drove benchmark cracks to historic levels in the third quarter, and we were well-positioned to commercially to capture that value," he said on Cenovus' thirdquarter results call. Cenovus posted total refining revenues of C$8.6 billion in Q3, of which US refining revenues accounted for C$6.6 billion, reflecting higher refined product prices.
Tight global diesel markets propelled cracks to high levels. According to Platts data, the US Gulf Coast diesel crack -- a global benchmark given USGC refineries are the key supplier of the fuel worldwide -- the USGC ULSD prompt pipeline WTI crack averaged $87.39/b in Q3, up from the $59.30/b in Q2. In Q4, the crack is averaging $89.88/b as of Nov. 4. Platts is a unit of S&P Global.
Cenovus' Canadian downstream saw record crude throughput in Q3 of 112,000 b/d, or about 112% utilization with both the Lloydminster upgrader and refinery operating above rated capacity.
"Strong diesel pricing in the quarter also coincided with record diesel production of almost 18,000 b/d at the Lloydminster upgrader," McKenzie said, adding the strong fundamentals also supported premium pricing for synthetic oil given its high distillate yield.
During the Q3, Platts assessed Syncrude at a $9.71/b premium to WTI, up from $9.12/b in Q2. So far in Q4, the premium is averaging $8.21/b on Nov. 4.
In the US, Cenovus' larger refining presence reported Q3 crude throughput of 317,000 b/d, or about 87% utilization due to the turnaround at its Lima, Ohio, refinery which ran from early September to end October. Despite high margins, Cenovus elected to not defer maintenance until 2027 as some of its peers have done. Regardless, market capture rose to 88% in Q3 compared to 67% in Q2.
Cenovus' US downstream operates in the Midwestern US, and includes a 44,000 b/d Superior, Wisconsin, refinery, which runs all Cenovus crude, and two Ohio refineries - Lima and Toledo.
McKenzie said part of the higher market capture was the ability to move refined product out of the Midwest into the US Atlantic Coast on recently expanded capacity of the Laurel Pipe Line, which stretches across Pennsylvania connecting the Midwest with the US Atlantic Coast.

"In September, we began utilizing our share of the 40,000 b/d expansion on the Laurel Pipe Line, providing another important outlet for products from the Chicago market, " McKenzie said.
"In combination with our continued movement from the dock in Toledo, we have increased our ability to access higher-value markets in PADD 1 and the Great Lakes region," he added.
The Toledo dock saw record performance in Q3, according to Eric Zimpfer, head of Cenovus' downstream, as Cenovus was able to find the right market to get the highest netback for its refined products.
"That's both the Toledo and Lima refineries to make sure we're placing products into the market that are most advantaged," he said on the call, adding Cenovus was looking at a "number of investments" to continue to improve the facility.
Given the tight global diesel market, Cenovus is looking to improve yields at its two Ohio refineries.
"We're looking at how do we improve our yields and how do we get more distillate, jet and diesel yields. How do we continue to optimize across the Ohio Valley region and really unlock some of the synergies that we see...and are pretty excited about," Zimpfer said.
He provided no details as to the scope and timeline of what projects the company was considering to achieve that goal.
-- Janet Mcgurty
REFINERY NEWS: Uzbekistan's Bukhara plans to add new units, expand capacity
Refinery: Bukhara, Uzbekistan Owner: Uzbekneftegaz Overall capacity: 2.5 million mt/year (50,000 b/d)
Uzbekistan's Bukhara refinery plans to build new units and expand its capacity as part of its modernization, according to a statement on the country's President's website Nov. 3. President Shavkat Mirziyoyev was presented with the modernization plans during his visit to the refinery Nov. 3.
The upgrade plans include building a naphtha hydrodesulfurization and isomerization unit, which will enable the refinery to increase the production of 95 and 92 RON gasoline that meet Euro 5 standards.

According to previous information, the refinery's modernization plans include a naphtha hydrotreater with 360,000 metric tons/year capacity, an isomerization unit with 380,000 mt/ year capacity and a selective hydrogenation of pyrolysis distillate unit with 60,000 mt/year capacity.
Improvements to feedstock fractionation and the deep processing of heavy residues are also part of the plans, which will help Bukhara double its capacity from 2.5 million mt/year to 5 million mt/year, thereby increasing domestic supply and exports, according to the statement.

REFINERY NEWS: Pemex reports coker breaker trip at Deer Park, Texas, plant
Refinery: Deer Park, Texas
Owner: Pemex
Overall capacity: 312,500 b/d
Units affected: Coker
Duration: Nov. 3, 1:53-3:08 pm CT
Mexico's state oil company Pemex reported an emissions event at its Deer Park refinery in Texas after a third-party breaker tripped and caused the loss of multiple pieces of equipment, according to a Nov. 4 filing with the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality.
The event was discovered Nov. 3 at 1:53 pm and ended the same day at 3:08 pm, the filing showed. The incident involved the 90,000 barrels/day delayed coking unit and sulfur recovery units 5, 6 and 7.
Immediately following the incident, prices of USGC pipeline ULSD rose about 3% to $4.50/gal on Nov. 3, up from $4.37/gal on Nov. 2, according to assessments by Platts, part of S&P Global

Energy. As of Nov. 4, the price was 7% lower day over day at $4.17/gal.
According to the TCEQ filing, specialists responded to evaluate the situation, assess the impact to affected equipment and mitigate operational disruptions to the greatest extent possible, the company said.
The cause of the breaker trip is under investigation. -- Janet Mcgurty

Platts launches LR1 Persian Gulf-Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment Nov 2, 2026
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has launched a new Long Range I {LR1} Persian Gulf to Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
This decision follows extensive industry consultations and analysis of spot fixtures and market data available since 2019, as well as direct feedback from market participants.
An analysis of spot fixtures on the key routes from the Persian Gulf to Singapore and from West Coast India to Singapore indicates that increasingly more concluded spot fixtures were based on 60kt cargo sizes. Over 2019-2025, on the Persian Gulf-to-Singapore route, 77 observed spot fixtures were concluded on 60kt cargoes in the LR1 class of clean tankers. On the West Coast India-to-Singapore route, 223 spot fixtures were concluded for 60kt cargoes over the same period.
LR1 vessels typically load between 55,000 mt and 65,000 mt of clean product, including naphtha, gasoline, jet fuel, and gasoil. Platts has observed LR1 vessels frequently being fixed on a spot basis on the Persian Gulf-Singapore route using 60kt stems, particularly for shipments of jet fuel and gasoil.
The new assessment reflects a 60kt clean product cargo loading from the Persian Gulf and discharging in Singapore, using modern LR1 tonnage with an age below 15 years. Platts will assess the route based on observed fixtures, as well as vessels going on subjects, bids/offers, and freight indications obtained through market survey of shipowners, charterers, and shipbrokers.
The assessment is published on a Worldscale percentage basis, together with a corresponding price in $/mt. The $/mt figure is derived from a flat rate based on a basket of key load and discharge ports.
The route basket consists of: Jubail-Singapore Ruwais-Singapore Ras Tanura-Singapore The basket flat rate used to calculate the $/mt freight will be based on the average of the Worldscale flat rates for the three routes. Platts will continue to assess its existing suite of benchmark Clean Persian Gulf-Japan 55kt LR1 assessments. The new assessments appear in Clean Tankerwire, Monthly Clean Tankerwire, Platts Shipping Alert pages 330 and 331, and Platts Tanker Alert pages 2922, and 2923. Assessments follow a 16:30 Singapore timestamp and the Singapore holiday calendar. Please send any feedback or questions to shipping@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts amends freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has amended the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts is using the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), launched Aug. 3, to calculate the outright prices of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).

The change better reflects shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah and Oman from Mina Al Fahal outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Previously, the freight calculation was based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026, followed by a decision on Oct. 15, 2026.
The freight calculation continues to use the metric ton-tobarrel conversion factors applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts is also using the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East Worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices (MPP).
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This was previously calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

The Platts Jet Fuel Index is calculated using daily assessments of Jet fuel spot prices in relevant regional centers. These values are compared with average spot prices in the base period (Index value of year 2000 = 100%) to generate a percentage figure reflecting the overall rise or fall in markets compared to the base period.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
